<browser_instruction># Claude in Chrome browser automation

You have access to browser automation tools (mcp__claude-in-chrome__*) for interacting with web pages in Chrome. Follow these guidelines for effective browser automation.

## Loading deferred tools

If the mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools are deferred (must be loaded via ToolSearch before use), load every tool you expect to need in ONE ToolSearch call — the select query accepts a comma-separated list — never one call per tool. Start with the core set:

ToolSearch with query "select:mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__navigate,mcp__claude-in-chrome__computer,mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_page,mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_close_mcp"

Add task-specific tools to the same call when the task obviously needs them: read_console_messages / read_network_requests for debugging, form_input for forms, gif_creator for recordings, javascript_tool for page scripting.

## GIF recording

When performing multi-step browser interactions that the user may want to review or share, use mcp__claude-in-chrome__gif_creator to record them.

You must ALWAYS:
* Capture extra frames before and after taking actions to ensure smooth playback
* Name the file meaningfully to help the user identify it later (e.g., "login_process.gif")

## Console log debugging

You can use mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages to read console output. Console output may be verbose. If you are looking for specific log entries, use the 'pattern' parameter with a regex-compatible pattern. This filters results efficiently and avoids overwhelming output. For example, use pattern: "[MyApp]" to filter for application-specific logs rather than reading all console output.

## Alerts and dialogs

IMPORTANT: Do not trigger JavaScript alerts, confirms, prompts, or browser modal dialogs through your actions. These browser dialogs block all further browser events and will prevent the extension from receiving any subsequent commands. Instead, when possible, use console.log for debugging and then use the mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages tool to read those log messages. If a page has dialog-triggering elements:
1. Avoid clicking buttons or links that may trigger alerts (e.g., "Delete" buttons with confirmation dialogs)
2. If you must interact with such elements, warn the user first that this may interrupt the session
3. Use mcp__claude-in-chrome__javascript_tool to check for and dismiss any existing dialogs before proceeding

If you accidentally trigger a dialog and lose responsiveness, inform the user they need to manually dismiss it in the browser.

## Avoid rabbit holes and loops

When using browser automation tools, stay focused on the specific task. If you encounter any of the following, stop and ask the user for guidance:
- Unexpected complexity or tangential browser exploration
- Browser tool calls failing or returning errors after 2-3 attempts
- No response from the browser extension
- Page elements not responding to clicks or input
- Pages not loading or timing out
- Unable to complete the browser task despite multiple approaches

Explain what you attempted, what went wrong, and ask how the user would like to proceed. Do not keep retrying the same failing browser action or explore unrelated pages without checking in first.

## Tab context and session startup

IMPORTANT: At the start of each browser automation session, call mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp first to get information about the user's current browser tabs. Use this context to understand what the user might want to work with before creating new tabs.

Never reuse tab IDs from a previous/other session. Follow these guidelines:
1. Only reuse an existing tab if the user explicitly asks to work with it
2. Otherwise, create a new tab with mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp
3. If a tool returns an error indicating the tab doesn't exist or is invalid, call tabs_context_mcp to get fresh tab IDs
4. When a tab is closed by the user or a navigation error occurs, call tabs_context_mcp to see what tabs are available</browser_instruction>

1	# SC2002 Restaurant Rush
2	
3	A turn-based restaurant management game for the command line, written in Java for the SC2002 group project.
4	The player is the manager: staff time is limited, so the manager decides which customer or order comes first.
5	
6	Repository: https://github.com/RobotHanzo/SC2002GroupProject
7	
8	## Build and run
9	
10	Needs JDK 17 or newer. No other dependencies, no network. Run every command from the repository root.
11	
12	```
13	javac --release 17 -d out -sourcepath src src/restaurantrush/Main.java
14	java -cp out restaurantrush.Main
15	```
16	
17	Replay a recorded run instead of typing the choices (the file is read and echoed, so the screen shows a transcript):
18	
19	```
20	java -cp out restaurantrush.Main --input runs/victory.txt
21	java -cp out restaurantrush.Main --input runs/defeat.txt
22	```
23	
24	Run the automated checks (see [docs/test-notes.md](docs/test-notes.md) for what they cover and for the expected results of both runs):
25	
26	```
27	javac --release 17 -d out -sourcepath src test/restaurantrush/GameTests.java
28	java -cp out restaurantrush.GameTests
29	```
30	
31	## Stage tags
32	
33	Each stage is a separate tagged commit that builds and runs on its own. `runs/` and the tests in each tag belong to that stage, so the recorded runs of an earlier stage are in its tag (`git checkout stage-1`).
34	
35	| Tag | Adds |
36	|---|---|
37	| `stage-1` | Basic operation: Regular customers, menu, Waiter, Chef, Cashier, Table, Order, the waiting queue, the full six-step turn, abandonment, victory and defeat |
38	| `stage-2` | Customer behaviour: VIP and Critic customers next to the Regulars |
39	| `stage-3` | New role and menu policy: the Host, ComboMeal and Happy Hour |
40	
41	## How to play
42	
43	The base setting is fixed: 18 turns, 2 tables, one Waiter, one Chef, one Cashier, one Host (Stage 3), and one new customer at the start of turns 1, 4, 7, 10, 13 and 16.
44	
45	| Menu entry | Price | Preparation units |
46	|---|---|---|
47	| Salad | $9.00 | 1 |
48	| Burger | $15.00 | 1 |
49	| Pasta | $18.00 | 2 |
50	| Salad + Burger (combo) | $21.60 | 2 |
51	| Salad + Pasta (combo) | $24.30 | 3 |
52	
53	A combo is made of two existing menu items: its price is 90% of the two prices added together (rounded to the nearest cent) and its preparation units are the sum. Customers pick their own food; the manager never does.
54	
55	| # | Customer | Arrives | Loses per waiting turn | Bill | Orders |
56	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
57	| 1 | VIP | turn 1 | 5 | 10% off | Burger |
58	| 2 | Regular | turn 4 | 8 | full price | Pasta |
59	| 3 | Regular | turn 7 | 8 | full price | Salad + Burger |
60	| 4 | Critic | turn 10 | 12 | full price | Salad + Pasta (the most expensive entry); loses a further 10 satisfaction if served more than 2 turns after the order was taken |
61	| 5 | Regular | turn 13 | 8 | full price | Salad |
62	| 6 | Regular | turn 16 | 8 | full price | Burger |
63	
64	Every turn the screen shows the turn number, revenue, the average satisfaction of paid customers, Happy Hour (and its remaining duration), the waiting queue, the tables, every customer's type, satisfaction and order status, and the tasks each staff member can do. Then you decide:
65	
66	| Prompt | Commands | What happens |
67	|---|---|---|
68	| Happy Hour (only while unused) | `y`, `n` (or Enter) | `y` switches Happy Hour on for this turn and the next; it can be used once per game |
69	| Host (one task per turn) | `SEAT <#>`, `WAIT` | seat a queued customer at a free table, in addition to the Waiter's task |
70	| Waiter (one task per turn) | `SEAT <#>`, `TAKE_ORDER <#>`, `SERVE <#>`, `WAIT` | seat a queued customer, take a seated customer's order, or serve a READY order |
71	| Chef (one unit per turn) | `COOK <#>`, `WAIT` | advance a customer's order by one preparation unit |
72	
73	`<#>` is the customer number shown on screen (`#2` and `2` both work). It may be left out when only one customer qualifies. A blank line means `WAIT`. A choice that cannot be done is explained and you choose again; it does not use up the staff task.
74	
75	After your choices the turn finishes on its own: customers who were served earlier finish eating (READY_TO_PAY), the Cashier charges at most one customer who became READY_TO_PAY on an earlier turn, and every customer who is still waiting for service loses satisfaction (the amount depends on the customer type). A customer who reaches 0 leaves without paying, the order is cancelled and the table is freed.
76	
77	After turn 18 you win if at least 4 customers have paid, revenue is at least $45.00 and the average satisfaction of the customers who paid is at least 60. Otherwise you lose. The game never ends early.
78	
79	### Happy Hour
80	
81	Switched on at the start of a turn, it lasts for that turn and the next, and the screen shows the turns left. While it is on:
82	
83	- orders taken get 20% off their bill. The VIP discount is applied first, then Happy Hour: a VIP's Burger is $15.00 x 0.9 x 0.8 = $10.80; a Regular's Pasta is $14.40;
84	- at the start of each active turn, customers still waiting for a table, to order, or for food recover 15 satisfaction (never above 100).
85	
86	The bill is locked when the order is taken, so Happy Hour never changes an order already placed and never touches customers who have left.
87	
88	### Rules this implementation fixes where the brief leaves a choice
89	
90	- **Food choice.** Regular and VIP customers take menu entry number `customerId mod 5` (the menu has five entries: Salad 0, Burger 1, Pasta 2, Salad + Burger 3, Salad + Pasta 4), so customer 1 orders a Burger, 2 a Pasta, 3 the Salad + Burger combo, 4 the Salad + Pasta combo, 5 a Salad. The Critic ignores that rule and takes the most expensive entry. The choice is shown when the order is taken.
91	- **Price lock.** The bill is worked out when the Waiter takes the order (customer's own pricing first, then Happy Hour) and is stored in the Order; the Cashier collects that amount.
92	- **Critic reaction.** "Served more than 2 turns after the order was taken" means the serving turn minus the order turn is 3 or more. A 3-unit combo ordered on turn *n* is READY at the end of turn *n*+2 at the earliest and served on turn *n*+3, so the Critic's combo always costs the extra 10 satisfaction; with a 2-unit dish a prompt Waiter avoids it.
93	- **Order of events in a turn.** Arrival, Happy Hour choice and recovery, your Host choice, your Waiter choice, your Chef choice (so the Chef can work on an order taken this turn, and the Waiter can take the order of a customer the Host has just seated), eating and payment, then waiting and abandonment. A dish the Chef finishes this turn is READY only after the Waiter has acted, so it is served next turn at the earliest.
94	- **Eating and payment.** A customer served on turn *n* is READY_TO_PAY on turn *n*+1 and can pay on turn *n*+2. "Oldest first" means the customer who arrived first.
95	
96	## From stage-1 to stage-2
97	
98	Compare: https://github.com/RobotHanzo/SC2002GroupProject/compare/stage-1...stage-2
99	
100	The Stage 2 commits are `efec627`, `69a34cb`, `bc1cd9f` and `10f4b5e` plus the diagram and documentation commits after them.
101	
102	**New files**
103	
104	| File | Responsibility |
105	|---|---|
106	| `entity/Menu.java` | Owns the dishes and finds the most expensive one (the Critic needs it) |
107	| `entity/RegularCustomer.java` | Baseline customer: loses 8, full price |
108	| `entity/VIPCustomer.java` | Loses 5, bill is 90% of the list price |
109	| `entity/CriticCustomer.java` | Loses 12, orders the most expensive dish, upset by slow service |
110	| `control/ArrivalSchedule.java` | Creates the six base-setting arrivals and decides their type (VIP on turn 1, Critic on turn 10) |
111	
112	**Existing files changed, and why**
113	
114	| File | Change | Reason |
115	|---|---|---|
116	| `entity/Customer.java` | Became abstract; added the hooks `getTypeLabel`, `getSatisfactionLoss`, `calculateBillCents`, `onServed` and `adjustSatisfaction`; `chooseMenuItem` takes a `Menu`; the fixed loss of 8 moved to `RegularCustomer` | Customer types must differ without the controller testing the type. All turn guards (`canFinishEating`, `canPay`) and the status rules are untouched |
117	| `entity/Order.java` | Constructor takes the bill and the turn it was taken; `getPriceCents()` became `getBillCents()` | The VIP discount means the price is no longer the item's list price, so the bill is locked in the order when it is taken. The status transitions are untouched |
118	| `entity/Waiter.java` | `takeOrder` takes a `Menu` and the turn, asks the customer for the dish and the bill, and returns the `Order`; `serveOrder` calls `customer.onServed` | This is where type-specific behaviour is dispatched: the Waiter calls the methods on `Customer` and never checks the type |
119	| `entity/Cashier.java` | Reads `getBillCents()` | Follows the Order change; one line |
120	| `entity/Restaurant.java` | Holds a `Menu` instead of a list; `registerArrival(Customer)` receives the customer instead of creating one | The schedule now decides which kind of customer arrives |
121	| `control/GameController.java` | Holds an `ArrivalSchedule` and asks it for arrivals; passes the `Menu` and turn to the Waiter; messages show the customer type | Registration of the new classes. The six-step turn, the guards and the result rule are unchanged |
122	| `boundary/RestaurantCLI.java` | Shows the type, and the locked bill of each order | Display only |
123	| `runs/victory.txt`, `runs/defeat.txt` | Replaced | The base-setting arrivals now include a VIP and a Critic, so the Stage 1 inputs no longer fit. The Stage 1 runs are still in the `stage-1` tag |
124	| `test/restaurantrush/GameTests.java` | Extended | See below |
125	
126	**Unchanged:** `Chef`, `Table`, `MenuItem`, `CustomerStatus`, `OrderStatus`, `Money`, `Main`.
127	
128	**Deleted:** nothing.
129	
130	**What still works.** `GameTests` keeps the Stage 1 checks that still apply (menu, order status rules, the Regular's loss of 8, the turn guards for eating and payment, the Table, payment and abandonment), rewritten only where a constructor changed, and adds the Stage 2 checks. It also checks that `GameController` contains no `instanceof` and never names a customer type.
131	
132	## From stage-2 to stage-3
133	
134	Compare: https://github.com/RobotHanzo/SC2002GroupProject/compare/stage-2...stage-3
135	
136	The Stage 3 feature commits are `d6fbec7`, `0231d21`, `9ed8d12`, `9225d1b`, `7d210d3` and `a6e64d3`. They are followed by the diagram and documentation commits and by one clean-up commit, `74c50eb`, with its diagram commit `ba84062` (see "Stage 3 clean-up" below).
137	
138	**New files**
139	
140	| File | Responsibility |
141	|---|---|
142	| `entity/ComboMeal.java` | A menu item made of two existing menu items (composition); computes the 90% price and the summed preparation units |
143	| `entity/HappyHour.java` | The pricing policy: once per game, two turns, 20% off new orders, +15 satisfaction for waiting customers |
144	| `entity/Host.java` | The new staff role: seats one queued customer per turn on top of the Waiter's task |
145	| `control/ManagerCommand.java` | Clean-up: reads one typed manager choice (a verb and an optional customer number) and picks the customer it refers to |
146	| `control/StaffActions.java` | Clean-up: carries out a Host, Waiter or Chef command and returns the sentence to show; an impossible command throws before anything changes |
147	
148	**Existing files changed, and why**
149	
150	| File | Change | Reason |
151	|---|---|---|
152	| `entity/MenuItem.java` | No longer `final` (one word) | `ComboMeal` extends it, so Waiter, Chef, Cashier, Menu and Order treat a combo like any dish and need no change |
153	| `entity/Customer.java` | Added `recoverSatisfaction`; `isAwaitingService()` became public; clean-up: added `getDisplayName()` | Happy Hour recovery and the controller select customers who are still waiting. The existing satisfaction clamp (`adjustSatisfaction`) is reused. `getDisplayName()` gives every message one way to name a customer |
154	| `entity/Waiter.java` | `takeOrder` also takes the `HappyHour` and applies it after the customer's own price | Happy Hour is applied after the VIP discount and locked in the Order. Seating, serving and the Critic hook are untouched |
155	| `entity/Restaurant.java` | Owns a `Host` and a `HappyHour` (with getters); the menu now lists two combos. Clean-up: `getWaitingQueue()` is read-only and `seatFromQueue` is the only way out of the queue; `getCustomersWithStatus`, `getSeatableCustomers`, `getCustomersToServe` and `getCustomersToCook` say what each staff member can do now; the three victory targets and `hasMetObjective()` | Registration of the new classes and of the combos, once, at set-up. The clean-up gives the queue and the staff-task rules one owner: the controller used to remove customers from the live queue itself, and the screen and the controller each worked out what a staff member can do. Payment and abandonment logic is untouched |
156	| `entity/Order.java` | Clean-up: removed the unused `orderId` and its static counter | Nothing read the id, and a static counter is global state shared by every game. Status rules and the locked bill are untouched |
157	| `control/GameController.java` | New steps before the staff choices: Happy Hour activation and recovery, and the Host choice; `HappyHour.endTurn()` at the end of the turn; the waiting loop uses `isAwaitingService()`. Clean-up: command parsing and task execution moved to `ManagerCommand` and `StaffActions`; the result check moved to `Restaurant.hasMetObjective()` | The Stage 3 turn needs the new steps. The clean-up leaves the controller sequencing the six steps (324 to 150 lines). The Waiter and Chef logic, payment and result rule are unchanged |
158	| `boundary/RestaurantCLI.java` | Host and Happy Hour prompts, Happy Hour status line, Host task line. Clean-up: no longer references `GameController`; the turn number and targets are passed in; the task lines print what `Restaurant` reports | Display and input only. Boundary and control no longer depend on each other |
159	| `Main.java` | Clean-up: creates the CLI and the controller (`Main.play`) and handles the end of the input | So that `RestaurantCLI` does not need the controller |
160	| `runs/victory.txt`, `runs/defeat.txt` | Replaced | There are two more prompts (Happy Hour and Host), and the menu changes what customers order. The Stage 2 runs are in the `stage-2` tag |
161	| `test/restaurantrush/GameTests.java` | Extended | See below |
162	
163	**Unchanged:** `Menu`, `Table`, `Chef`, `Cashier`, `ArrivalSchedule`, `RegularCustomer`, `VIPCustomer`, `CriticCustomer`, the enums, `Money`.
164	
165	**Deleted:** nothing.
166	
167	**Stage 3 clean-up (no behaviour change).** After the Stage 3 features worked, review found structure the features did not need but the design should not keep: a controller that also parsed commands and picked customers, the screen repeating the staff-task rules, a live queue handed out for the controller to edit, a static order counter, and a controller and screen that referenced each other. The clean-up commit `74c50eb` fixes these in the files listed above and touches no rule: the Waiter, Host, Chef, Cashier, `HappyHour`, `ComboMeal` and the customer types are unchanged. Evidence that behaviour is the same: `runs/victory.txt`, `runs/defeat.txt` and an invalid-input session print byte-identical transcripts before and after, and every earlier check still passes.
168	
169	**What still works.** `GameTests` keeps every Stage 1 and Stage 2 check (the Stage 1 checks that used the three-dish menu now build that menu explicitly, because the game menu has five entries) and adds the combo reference values, Happy Hour timing, discount, price lock and recovery, the Host, and (clean-up) the queue protection, staff task lists and objective check. The recorded runs replay the whole game. See [docs/test-notes.md](docs/test-notes.md).

193	## AI history
194

## 0023 User — 2026-11-08T18:44:09.686Z

Source record: 98; UUID: 98f36b5c-3c06-4d3c-83d9-de4c51f6ae1c.

### Tool result: toolu_01AhkXRpzwwTUyvMGhoos1pZ

1	SC2002 Restaurant Rush Group Assignment
2	Java command-line turn-based restaurant management game  |  Student brief
3	Your group builds a playable restaurant management game in Java. The player is the manager. Staff time is limited, so the manager decides which customers and orders come first. You build the game in three stages, and each stage must show how your object-oriented design supports the next set of requirements.
4	Item
5	Summary
6	What you build
7	A turn-based restaurant game with a command-line interface, in three runnable stages.
8	Stages
9	All three stages are published now and submitted together. Each stage is a tagged, runnable version in GitHub.
10	Interface
11	A command-line interface is enough. A GUI is optional and earns no extra marks by itself.
12	Submission
13	One zip per group: source code, README, PDF report, recorded video and declarations (Section 6).
14	Assessment
15	No live demo. Marks come from your submitted files, GitHub repository and video (Section 7).
16	1  Learning outcomes
17	Assign clear responsibilities to objects and use encapsulation, inheritance, polymorphism and composition appropriately.
18	Explain how the design changed across three working stages using UML, code and GitHub evidence.
19	Implement a complete game with meaningful player choices, a clear result and robust input handling.
20	2  Game setup
21	2.1  Base setting
22	Your game must always offer this base setting, so that a TA can reproduce your runs. Other difficulty settings are optional.
23	Setting
24	Required base value
25	Turns
26	18 turns maximum
27	Tables
28	2
29	Staff
30	Waiter × 1 (one task per turn)
31	Chef × 1 (one preparation unit per turn)
32	Cashier × 1 (one payment per turn)
33	Arrivals
34	One customer at the start of turns 1, 4, 7, 10, 13 and 16 (six in total)
35	Objective
36	At the end of turn 18: at least 4 paid customers AND revenue at least $45 AND average satisfaction of paid customers at least 60
37	Menu item
38	Price
39	Preparation units
40	Salad
41	$9.00
42	1
43	Burger
44	$15.00
45	1
46	Pasta
47	$18.00
48	2
49	2.2  General rules
50	Customers choose their own food. The manager never chooses it. Use a documented deterministic rule or seeded randomness, and show the choice once the order is placed.
51	Keep rules out of the UI. The UI only collects the manager’s decisions and displays the state. Game rules belong in domain and control objects.
52	Money. If fractional prices arise, store amounts as integer cents. Display dollars with two decimals.
53	Offline. All mandatory features must work without network access.
54	3  How a turn works
55	3.1  Start-of-turn display
56	At the start of every turn, show: the turn number, revenue, average satisfaction of paid customers, the waiting queue, occupied tables, each customer’s satisfaction, order status, and the staff tasks available.
57	3.2  Turn steps
58	Each turn then runs these six steps in order.
59	Step
60	What happens
61	1  Arrivals
62	The scheduled customer joins the waiting queue with satisfaction 100.
63	2  Manager choices
64	Waiter: choose at most one task, or Wait: seat one queued customer at a free table, take one seated customer’s order, or serve one READY order.
65	Chef: choose at most one order to advance by one preparation unit, or Wait. The Chef may work on an order the Waiter took this turn.
66	3  Kitchen and serving
67	The Waiter acts before the Chef. When an order’s last preparation unit is done, it becomes READY. A dish can be served only if it was already READY when the Waiter acts, so a dish finished this turn is served next turn at the earliest. Serving sets it to SERVED.
68	4  Eating and payment
69	A customer served on an earlier turn finishes one full turn of eating and becomes READY_TO_PAY. The Cashier automatically handles at most one READY_TO_PAY customer per turn, oldest first. A customer who became READY_TO_PAY this turn pays next turn at the earliest. Payment adds the bill to revenue, marks the customer and order PAID, and frees the table.
70	5  Waiting and abandonment
71	At turn end, every waiting or seated customer who has not been served loses satisfaction (Regular: 8 points, minimum 0). A customer who reaches 0 leaves without paying: cancel any unfinished order and free the table. Customers who are served, eating or ready to pay lose nothing.
72	6  Result
73	The game always runs all 18 turns; there is no early ending. After turn 18: Victory if all three targets in Section 2.1 are met, otherwise Defeat.
74	3.3  Example: one customer from arrival to payment
75	This example follows one Regular customer who orders Pasta (2 preparation units) and gets the manager’s full attention.
76	Turn
77	What happens
78	Satisfaction at turn end
79	1
80	Arrives (100). Waiter seats the customer.
81	92 (seated, not served)
82	2
83	Waiter takes the Pasta order. Chef does unit 1 of 2.
84	84
85	3
86	Chef does unit 2 of 2: order is READY. The Waiter cannot serve it this turn.
87	76
88	4
89	Waiter serves: SERVED.
90	76 (no further loss)
91	5
92	Customer finishes eating: READY_TO_PAY.
93	76
94	6
95	Cashier collects $18.00: PAID, table freed. Satisfaction 76 is recorded.
96	—
97	3.4  Rules that always apply
98	A Waiter completes at most one task per turn.
99	A table stays occupied until its customer pays or leaves.
100	A customer cannot eat before being served. The Cashier cannot charge before READY_TO_PAY.
101	An invalid choice must explain why it failed and let the manager choose again, without using up the staff task.
102	Revenue never includes unpaid or abandoned orders.
103	The customer who arrives on turn 16 cannot finish paying within 18 turns, so at most five customers can pay. This is intended, not a bug.
104	3.5  End-of-turn results
105	Show the served count, unhappy departures, revenue, average satisfaction of paid customers, and turns used. The average uses the satisfaction recorded when each customer pays. If no one has paid yet, show N/A.
106	4  The three stages
107	Each stage is a separate runnable version tagged in GitHub, and each builds on the one before.
108	Stage
109	Adds
110	Git tag
111	1  Basic operation
112	Regular customers, menu, Waiter, Chef, Cashier, full turn sequence, both endings
113	stage-1
114	2  Customer behaviour
115	VIP and Critic customers
116	stage-2
117	3  New role and menu policy
118	Host, ComboMeal, Happy Hour
119	stage-3
120	4.1  Stage 1: Basic restaurant operation
121	Build a complete playable game containing:
122	regular customers and the three menu items;
123	Waiter, Chef, Cashier, Table, Order and the waiting queue;
124	the full turn sequence, including satisfaction, abandonment, payment, victory and defeat.
125	The player sets seating, ordering and serving priority through the Waiter, and kitchen priority through the Chef.
126	Stage 1 must: compile and run on its own at tag stage-1; contain no Stage 2 or Stage 3 features; include one reproducible victory run and one reproducible defeat run in your test notes.
127	4.2  Stage 2: Customer behaviour extension
128	Add VIPCustomer and CriticCustomer alongside regular customers.
129	Customer
130	Satisfaction Loss per waiting turn
131	Bill
132	Special behaviour
133	Regular
134	8
135	Full price
136	—
137	VIP
138	5
139	10% off
140	—
141	Critic
142	12
143	Full price
144	A distinct order preference or reaction to service that you define and document
145	A reproducible base-setting run must include at least one VIP and one Critic. You may replace two of the regular arrivals; there must still be six arrivals.
146	All customer types follow the same order and service flow.
147	Different behaviour must be dispatched through the customer objects, not through a chain of type checks in the turn controller.
148	Stage 2 is independently playable and uses the same win and lose criteria.
149	4.3  Stage 3: New role and menu policy
150	Host. The Host seats at most one queued customer per turn, in addition to the Waiter’s task. The Waiter can still seat customers.
151	ComboMeal. A combo is made of two existing menu items.
152	Preparation units = the sum of its two components.
153	Price = 90% of the components’ combined price, rounded to the nearest cent. Example: Salad + Pasta = ($9 + $18) × 0.9 = $24.30, 3 units.
154	A customer chooses a combo according to a rule you document.
155	Happy Hour. A pricing policy the manager may switch on.
156	Can be activated once per game, at the start of a turn. It lasts for that turn and the next. Display its remaining duration.
157	Orders taken while it is active get 20% off their bill.
158	During each active turn, customers still waiting for a table or food recover 15 satisfaction at turn start (maximum 100).
159	Apply the VIP discount first, then Happy Hour. The price is locked when the order is taken. Example: a VIP orders a Burger during Happy Hour: $15 × 0.9 × 0.8 = $10.80.
160	It does not change orders already placed, and does not affect customers who have left.
161	Stage 3 is independently playable.
162	4.4  Design freedom and changes to earlier code
163	You choose class names and design patterns. Do not create interfaces just to satisfy a checklist.
164	If a later stage needs you to modify an earlier class, explain why. Small registration or setup changes are expected.
165	If you broadly rewrite stable game rules, explain why and show that earlier behaviour still works.
166	Extra features earn credit only when they show sound design and do not replace required rules.
167	5  Design evidence
168	Include these in your PDF report and repository.
169	Evidence
170	Requirements
171	Class diagram
172	Final design; must match the Stage 3 code.
173	Show the principal entity classes, necessary control and boundary classes, relevant enums, key attributes and operations with visibility, inheritance, and associations with multiplicities where relevant.
174	Annotate meaningful examples of encapsulation, polymorphism and composition.
175	Sequence diagram
176	One complete scenario in which a manager choice changes an outcome.
177	Include: customer arrival and order; Waiter and Chef assignments; READY then SERVED; eating; payment; the result check; one Stage 2 or 3 behaviour (e.g. VIP pricing or Happy Hour).
178	Show messages among the CLI, controller, staff and domain objects, with relevant guards and loops.
179	Design discussion
180	A table of key abstractions and their responsibilities.
181	SRP, OCP, LSP, ISP and DIP, only where genuinely reflected in your code.
182	Trade-offs, and one alternative design you considered.
183	How the player’s choices change a result.
184	GitHub evidence
185	Tags stage-1, stage-2 and stage-3 on runnable commits.
186	In the README, for each transition (1 → 2 and 2 → 3): new files; existing files changed or deleted, with reasons; the relevant commit or compare link.
187	The source code itself, not only the README, must show the real stages.
188	6  Submission
189	6.1  What to submit
190	Submit one archive per group through the course assignment link by Nov. 15, 2026 11:59pm. Name it LABGroup_AssignmentGrp.zip, for example FCS1_Grp1.zip.

1	package restaurantrush.control;
2	
3	import java.util.ArrayList;
4	import java.util.List;
5	import java.util.Optional;
6	import java.util.function.Function;
7	import java.util.function.Supplier;
8	
9	import restaurantrush.boundary.RestaurantCLI;
10	import restaurantrush.entity.Customer;
11	import restaurantrush.entity.HappyHour;
12	import restaurantrush.entity.Money;
13	import restaurantrush.entity.Restaurant;
14	
15	/**
16	 * Runs the game: 18 turns of six steps each (arrivals, manager choices, kitchen and serving,
17	 * eating and payment, waiting and abandonment, result). It owns the turn counter and the order of
18	 * the steps. Reading a choice is the CLI's job, carrying it out is StaffActions' job and deciding
19	 * whether it is legal is the domain objects' job; the controller only sequences them.
20	 */
21	public class GameController {
22	    private static final int MAX_TURNS = 18;
23	
24	    private final Restaurant restaurant;
25	    private final ArrivalSchedule schedule;
26	    private final StaffActions staff;
27	    private int currentTurn;
28	    private final int maxTurns;
29	    private final RestaurantCLI cli;
30	
31	    public GameController(RestaurantCLI cli) {
32	        this.cli = cli;
33	        this.restaurant = new Restaurant();
34	        this.schedule = new ArrivalSchedule();
35	        this.staff = new StaffActions(restaurant);
36	        this.maxTurns = MAX_TURNS;
37	    }
38	
39	    /** Plays all turns (there is no early ending), then reports victory or defeat. */
40	    public void startGame() {
41	        cli.displayIntro(maxTurns);
42	        while (currentTurn < maxTurns) {
43	            currentTurn++;
44	            executeTurn();
45	        }
46	        cli.displayResult(restaurant.hasMetObjective(), restaurant, currentTurn);
47	    }
48	
49	    private void executeTurn() {
50	        processScheduledArrival();
51	        cli.displayTurnStatus(restaurant, currentTurn, maxTurns);
52	        processHappyHourActivation();
53	        applyHappyHourRecovery();
54	        coordinateHostAction();
55	        coordinateStaffActions();
56	        coordinatePaymentProcessing();
57	        processEndOfTurnUpdates();
58	    }
59	
60	    /** Step 1: the schedule decides who arrives at the start of turns 1, 4, 7, 10, 13 and 16. */
61	    private void processScheduledArrival() {
62	        if (schedule.isArrivalTurn(currentTurn)) {
63	            restaurant.registerArrival(schedule.createArrival(currentTurn));
64	        }
65	    }
66	
67	    /** Start of turn: while Happy Hour is unused the manager may switch it on (once per game). */
68	    private void processHappyHourActivation() {
69	        HappyHour happyHour = restaurant.getHappyHour();
70	        if (happyHour.isAvailable() && cli.readHappyHourChoice() && happyHour.activate()) {
71	            cli.displayMessage("Happy Hour is ON: orders taken now get a discount and waiting customers recover "
72	                    + "satisfaction. Turns left: " + happyHour.getRemainingTurns() + ".");
73	        }
74	    }
75	
76	    /** Start of an active Happy Hour turn: customers still waiting for a table or food recover satisfaction. */
77	    private void applyHappyHourRecovery() {
78	        HappyHour happyHour = restaurant.getHappyHour();
79	        if (!happyHour.isActive()) {
80	            return;
81	        }
82	        int amount = happyHour.getRecoveryAmount();
83	        List<String> changes = new ArrayList<>();
84	        for (Customer customer : restaurant.getCustomers()) {
85	            if (customer.isAwaitingService()) {
86	                int before = customer.getSatisfaction();
87	                customer.recoverSatisfaction(amount);
88	                changes.add("#" + customer.getId() + " " + before + " -> " + customer.getSatisfaction());
89	            }
90	        }
91	        cli.displayMessage("Happy Hour (" + happyHour.getRemainingTurns() + " turn(s) left including this one): "
92	                + (changes.isEmpty() ? "nobody is waiting." : "waiting customers recover " + String.join(", ", changes) + "."));
93	    }
94	
95	    /** Step 2 (Host): at most one queued customer is seated, on top of the Waiter's own task. */
96	    private void coordinateHostAction() {
97	        chooseUntilAccepted(cli::readHostChoice, staff::host);
98	    }
99	
100	    /** Steps 2 and 3: the Waiter acts first, then the Chef. A rejected choice does not use up the task. */
101	    private void coordinateStaffActions() {
102	        chooseUntilAccepted(cli::readWaiterChoice, command -> staff.waiter(command, currentTurn));
103	        chooseUntilAccepted(cli::readChefChoice, staff::chef);
104	    }
105	
106	    private void chooseUntilAccepted(Supplier<String> reader, Function<ManagerCommand, Optional<String>> task) {
107	        while (true) {
108	            String choice = reader.get();
109	            try {
110	                task.apply(ManagerCommand.parse(choice)).ifPresent(cli::displayMessage);
111	                return;
112	            } catch (IllegalArgumentException | IllegalStateException rejected) {
113	                cli.displayError(rejected.getMessage());
114	            }
115	        }
116	    }
117	
118	    /** Step 4: served customers finish eating, then the Cashier charges at most one READY_TO_PAY customer. */
119	    private void coordinatePaymentProcessing() {
120	        for (Customer customer : restaurant.getCustomers()) {
121	            if (customer.canFinishEating(currentTurn)) {
122	                customer.markReadyToPay(currentTurn);
123	                cli.displayMessage(customer.getDisplayName() + " finishes eating and is ready to pay.");
124	            }
125	        }
126	        Customer payer = restaurant.findOldestEligiblePayer(currentTurn);
127	        if (payer != null) {
128	            int bill = payer.getOrder().getBillCents();
129	            restaurant.getCashier().processPayment(payer, restaurant);
130	            cli.displayMessage("Cashier collects " + Money.format(bill) + " from " + payer.getDisplayName()
131	                    + " (satisfaction " + payer.getSatisfaction() + "). The table is free again.");
132	        }
133	    }
134	
135	    /** Step 5: customers who are still waiting lose satisfaction; at 0 they leave without paying. */
136	    private void processEndOfTurnUpdates() {
137	        for (Customer customer : restaurant.getCustomers()) {
138	            if (!customer.isAwaitingService()) {
139	                continue;
140	            }
141	            customer.reduceSatisfaction();
142	            if (customer.shouldAbandon()) {
143	                restaurant.handleAbandonment(customer);
144	                cli.displayMessage(customer.getDisplayName()
145	                        + " runs out of patience and leaves without paying. Any unfinished order is cancelled.");
146	            }
147	        }
148	        restaurant.getHappyHour().endTurn();
149	    }
150	}
151

1	package restaurantrush.entity;
2	
3	/**
4	 * A restaurant guest. Every kind of customer follows the same order and service flow; subclasses
5	 * differ only in the hooks below (loss per waiting turn, billing, food choice, reaction to being
6	 * served), so the controller and staff call these on Customer and never check the type.
7	 * Satisfaction and status are private and change only through the methods of this class.
8	 * Lifecycle: WAITING -> SEATED -> ORDERED -> SERVED -> READY_TO_PAY -> PAID, or ABANDONED.
9	 */
10	public abstract class Customer {
11	    private static final int MAX_SATISFACTION = 100;
12	
13	    private final int customerId;
14	    private int satisfaction = MAX_SATISFACTION;
15	    private CustomerStatus status = CustomerStatus.WAITING;
16	    private Order order;
17	    private int servedTurn;
18	    private int readyTurn;
19	
20	    protected Customer(int customerId) {
21	        this.customerId = customerId;
22	    }
23	
24	    public int getId() {
25	        return customerId;
26	    }
27	
28	    public CustomerStatus getStatus() {
29	        return status;
30	    }
31	
32	    public int getSatisfaction() {
33	        return satisfaction;
34	    }
35	
36	    /** Short name of the customer type, for display. */
37	    public abstract String getTypeLabel();
38	
39	    /** How messages refer to this customer, for example "Customer #3 (Regular)". */
40	    public String getDisplayName() {
41	        return "Customer #" + customerId + " (" + getTypeLabel() + ")";
42	    }
43	
44	    /** Satisfaction lost at the end of each turn spent waiting for service. */
45	    protected abstract int getSatisfactionLoss();
46	
47	    /** Documented food rule: menu entry number (customerId mod menu size). The Critic overrides it. */
48	    public MenuItem chooseMenuItem(Menu menu) {
49	        return menu.getItem(customerId % menu.size());
50	    }
51	
52	    /** The bill for a dish at the given list price. The VIP overrides it to apply a discount. */
53	    public int calculateBillCents(int basePriceCents) {
54	        return basePriceCents;
55	    }
56	
57	    /** Called when the Waiter serves this customer; turnsWaited counts turns since the order was taken. */
58	    public void onServed(int turnsWaited) {
59	    }
60	
61	    /** Only subclasses may move satisfaction directly; the value always stays within 0..100. */
62	    protected void adjustSatisfaction(int delta) {
63	        satisfaction = Math.max(0, Math.min(MAX_SATISFACTION, satisfaction + delta));
64	    }
65	
66	    /** End-of-turn loss for a customer who has not been served yet; everyone else loses nothing. */
67	    public void reduceSatisfaction() {
68	        if (isAwaitingService()) {
69	            adjustSatisfaction(-getSatisfactionLoss());
70	        }
71	    }
72	
73	    /** Happy Hour: a customer who is still waiting for a table or food regains satisfaction (never above 100). */
74	    public void recoverSatisfaction(int amount) {
75	        if (isAwaitingService()) {
76	            adjustSatisfaction(amount);
77	        }
78	    }
79	
80	    public void assignOrder(Order newOrder) {
81	        require(CustomerStatus.SEATED);
82	        order = newOrder;
83	        status = CustomerStatus.ORDERED;
84	    }
85	
86	    public Order getOrder() {
87	        return order;
88	    }
89	
90	    public void markSeated() {
91	        require(CustomerStatus.WAITING);
92	        status = CustomerStatus.SEATED;
93	    }
94	
95	    public void markServed(int turn) {
96	        require(CustomerStatus.ORDERED);
97	        servedTurn = turn;
98	        status = CustomerStatus.SERVED;
99	    }
100	
101	    /** A served customer eats for one full turn: allowed from the turn after the serving turn. */
102	    public boolean canFinishEating(int turn) {
103	        return status == CustomerStatus.SERVED && turn > servedTurn;
104	    }
105	
106	    public void markReadyToPay(int turn) {
107	        if (!canFinishEating(turn)) {
108	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customerId + " has not finished eating yet");
109	        }
110	        readyTurn = turn;
111	        status = CustomerStatus.READY_TO_PAY;
112	    }
113	
114	    /** The Cashier may charge from the turn after the customer became READY_TO_PAY. */
115	    public boolean canPay(int turn) {
116	        return status == CustomerStatus.READY_TO_PAY && turn > readyTurn;
117	    }
118	
119	    public void markPaid() {
120	        require(CustomerStatus.READY_TO_PAY);
121	        status = CustomerStatus.PAID;
122	    }
123	
124	    public boolean shouldAbandon() {
125	        return isAwaitingService() && satisfaction <= 0;
126	    }
127	
128	    public void markAbandoned() {
129	        if (!isAwaitingService()) {
130	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customerId + " is " + status + " and cannot abandon");
131	        }
132	        status = CustomerStatus.ABANDONED;
133	    }
134	
135	    /** True while the customer is waiting for a table, to order, or for food (not yet served). */
136	    public boolean isAwaitingService() {
137	        return status == CustomerStatus.WAITING || status == CustomerStatus.SEATED
138	                || status == CustomerStatus.ORDERED;
139	    }
140	
141	    private void require(CustomerStatus expected) {
142	        if (status != expected) {
143	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customerId + " is " + status + ", not " + expected);
144	        }
145	    }
146	}

1	package restaurantrush.entity;
2	
3	import java.util.ArrayList;
4	import java.util.Collections;
5	import java.util.List;
6	import java.util.function.BiConsumer;
7	
8	/**
9	 * The restaurant owns its tables, staff and menu (composition) and keeps the books: the waiting
10	 * queue, every customer who has arrived, and the revenue. Customers are aggregated because they
11	 * arrive and leave. It also knows what each staff member can do right now (the getCustomers...
12	 * queries below), so the screen and the controller ask it instead of re-deriving the rules.
13	 */
14	public class Restaurant {
15	    public static final int REQUIRED_PAID_CUSTOMERS = 4;
16	    public static final int REQUIRED_REVENUE_CENTS = 4500;
17	    public static final int REQUIRED_AVERAGE_SATISFACTION = 60;
18	
19	    private static final int TABLE_COUNT = 2;
20	
21	    private final List<Table> tables = new ArrayList<>();
22	    private final List<Customer> customers = new ArrayList<>();
23	    private final List<Customer> waitingQueue = new ArrayList<>();
24	    private final Waiter waiter = new Waiter();
25	    private final Chef chef = new Chef();
26	    private final Cashier cashier = new Cashier();
27	    private final Host host = new Host();
28	    private final Menu menu;
29	    private final HappyHour happyHour = new HappyHour();
30	    private int revenueCents;
31	
32	    public Restaurant() {
33	        for (int id = 1; id <= TABLE_COUNT; id++) {
34	            tables.add(new Table(id));
35	        }
36	        MenuItem salad = new MenuItem("Salad", 900, 1);
37	        MenuItem burger = new MenuItem("Burger", 1500, 1);
38	        MenuItem pasta = new MenuItem("Pasta", 1800, 2);
39	        // The combos are registered once here; customers pick from the whole menu.
40	        menu = new Menu(List.of(salad, burger, pasta, new ComboMeal(salad, burger), new ComboMeal(salad, pasta)));
41	    }
42	
43	    /** A newly arrived customer joins the back of the waiting queue. */
44	    public void registerArrival(Customer customer) {
45	        customers.add(customer);
46	        waitingQueue.add(customer);
47	    }
48	
49	    /** The first free table, or null when both are occupied. */
50	    public Table findAvailableTable() {
51	        for (Table table : tables) {
52	            if (table.isAvailable()) {
53	                return table;
54	            }
55	        }
56	        return null;
57	    }
58	
59	    /** The READY_TO_PAY customer who arrived first and may be charged this turn, or null. */
60	    public Customer findOldestEligiblePayer(int turn) {
61	        for (Customer customer : customers) {
62	            if (customer.canPay(turn)) {
63	                return customer;
64	            }
65	        }
66	        return null;
67	    }
68	
69	    public Waiter getWaiter() {
70	        return waiter;
71	    }
72	
73	    public Chef getChef() {
74	        return chef;
75	    }
76	
77	    public Cashier getCashier() {
78	        return cashier;
79	    }
80	
81	    public Host getHost() {
82	        return host;
83	    }
84	
85	    public List<Table> getTables() {
86	        return Collections.unmodifiableList(tables);
87	    }
88	
89	    public Menu getMenu() {
90	        return menu;
91	    }
92	
93	    public HappyHour getHappyHour() {
94	        return happyHour;
95	    }
96	
97	    public List<Customer> getCustomers() {
98	        return Collections.unmodifiableList(customers);
99	    }
100	
101	    /** The waiting queue, first to arrive first. Read-only: a customer leaves it by being seated or by abandoning. */
102	    public List<Customer> getWaitingQueue() {
103	        return Collections.unmodifiableList(waitingQueue);
104	    }
105	
106	    /**
107	     * Seats a queued customer by the given staff action (the Waiter's or the Host's seatCustomer).
108	     * The customer leaves the queue only if the staff member accepts the request, so a rejected
109	     * seating changes nothing.
110	     */
111	    public void seatFromQueue(Customer customer, Table table, BiConsumer<Customer, Table> seatedBy) {
112	        if (!waitingQueue.contains(customer)) {
113	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customer.getId() + " is not in the waiting queue");
114	        }
115	        seatedBy.accept(customer, table);
116	        waitingQueue.remove(customer);
117	    }
118	
119	    /** Customers with the given status, in arrival order. */
120	    public List<Customer> getCustomersWithStatus(CustomerStatus status) {
121	        List<Customer> matching = new ArrayList<>();
122	        for (Customer customer : customers) {
123	            if (customer.getStatus() == status) {
124	                matching.add(customer);
125	            }
126	        }
127	        return matching;
128	    }
129	
130	    /** Whom the Host or the Waiter could seat now: the queue, or nobody while both tables are occupied. */
131	    public List<Customer> getSeatableCustomers() {
132	        return findAvailableTable() == null ? List.of() : List.copyOf(waitingQueue);
133	    }
134	
135	    /** Customers whose dish is READY and waiting for the Waiter to serve it. */
136	    public List<Customer> getCustomersToServe() {
137	        List<Customer> matching = new ArrayList<>();
138	        for (Customer customer : getCustomersWithStatus(CustomerStatus.ORDERED)) {
139	            if (customer.getOrder().isReady()) {
140	                matching.add(customer);
141	            }
142	        }
143	        return matching;
144	    }
145	
146	    /** Customers whose dish still needs the Chef's preparation units. */
147	    public List<Customer> getCustomersToCook() {
148	        List<Customer> matching = new ArrayList<>();
149	        for (Customer customer : getCustomersWithStatus(CustomerStatus.ORDERED)) {
150	            if (!customer.getOrder().isReady()) {
151	                matching.add(customer);
152	            }
153	        }
154	        return matching;
155	    }
156	
157	    /** Victory needs all three targets (checked after the last turn). */
158	    public boolean hasMetObjective() {
159	        return getPaidCustomerCount() >= REQUIRED_PAID_CUSTOMERS
160	                && getRevenueCents() >= REQUIRED_REVENUE_CENTS
161	                && getAverageSatisfaction() >= REQUIRED_AVERAGE_SATISFACTION;
162	    }
163	
164	    public void recordPayment(int amountCents) {
165	        if (amountCents < 0) {
166	            throw new IllegalArgumentException("A payment cannot be negative");
167	        }
168	        revenueCents += amountCents;
169	    }
170	
171	    public void releaseTableOf(Customer customer) {
172	        for (Table table : tables) {
173	            if (table.getOccupied() == customer) {
174	                table.release();
175	            }
176	        }
177	    }
178	
179	    public int getPaidCustomerCount() {
180	        return countWithStatus(CustomerStatus.PAID);
181	    }
182	
183	    public int getServedCustomerCount() {
184	        return countWithStatus(CustomerStatus.SERVED) + countWithStatus(CustomerStatus.READY_TO_PAY)
185	                + countWithStatus(CustomerStatus.PAID);
186	    }
187	
188	    /** Mean satisfaction of paid customers, as recorded when each paid; 0 when nobody has paid. */
189	    public double getAverageSatisfaction() {
190	        int total = 0;
191	        int paid = 0;
192	        for (Customer customer : customers) {
193	            if (customer.getStatus() == CustomerStatus.PAID) {
194	                total += customer.getSatisfaction();
195	                paid++;
196	            }
197	        }
198	        return paid == 0 ? 0.0 : (double) total / paid;
199	    }
200	
201	    public int getRevenueCents() {
202	        return revenueCents;
203	    }
204	
205	    /** A customer whose satisfaction hit 0 leaves: cancel the unfinished order, free the table, leave the queue. */
206	    public void handleAbandonment(Customer customer) {
207	        customer.markAbandoned();
208	        Order order = customer.getOrder();
209	        if (order != null) {
210	            order.cancel();
211	        }
212	        releaseTableOf(customer);
213	        waitingQueue.remove(customer);
214	    }
215	
216	    public int getUnhappyDepartureCount() {
217	        return countWithStatus(CustomerStatus.ABANDONED);
218	    }
219	
220	    private int countWithStatus(CustomerStatus status) {
221	        int count = 0;
222	        for (Customer customer : customers) {
223	            if (customer.getStatus() == status) {
224	                count++;
225	            }
226	        }
227	        return count;
228	    }
229	}

1	package restaurantrush.control;
2	
3	import java.util.Optional;
4	import java.util.function.BiConsumer;
5	
6	import restaurantrush.entity.Customer;
7	import restaurantrush.entity.CustomerStatus;
8	import restaurantrush.entity.Money;
9	import restaurantrush.entity.MenuItem;
10	import restaurantrush.entity.Order;
11	import restaurantrush.entity.Restaurant;
12	import restaurantrush.entity.Table;
13	
14	/**
15	 * Carries out a manager's command with the Host, the Waiter or the Chef. Each method picks the
16	 * customer the command refers to, asks the staff member to do the task and returns the sentence
17	 * for the screen (empty for WAIT). An impossible command throws before anything is changed, so
18	 * the controller can ask again without the staff member having used the task.
19	 */
20	final class StaffActions {
21	    private final Restaurant restaurant;
22	
23	    StaffActions(Restaurant restaurant) {
24	        this.restaurant = restaurant;
25	    }
26	
27	    Optional<String> host(ManagerCommand command) {
28	        return switch (command.getVerb()) {
29	            case "WAIT" -> Optional.empty();
30	            case "SEAT" -> Optional.of(seat(command, restaurant.getHost()::seatCustomer, "Host"));
31	            default -> throw new IllegalArgumentException(
32	                    "Unknown Host command '" + command.getVerb() + "'. Use SEAT or WAIT.");
33	        };
34	    }
35	
36	    Optional<String> waiter(ManagerCommand command, int currentTurn) {
37	        return switch (command.getVerb()) {
38	            case "WAIT" -> Optional.empty();
39	            case "SEAT" -> Optional.of(seat(command, restaurant.getWaiter()::seatCustomer, "Waiter"));
40	            case "TAKE_ORDER", "ORDER", "TAKE" -> Optional.of(takeOrder(command, currentTurn));
41	            case "SERVE" -> Optional.of(serve(command, currentTurn));
42	            default -> throw new IllegalArgumentException(
43	                    "Unknown Waiter command '" + command.getVerb() + "'. Use SEAT, TAKE_ORDER, SERVE or WAIT.");
44	        };
45	    }
46	
47	    Optional<String> chef(ManagerCommand command) {
48	        return switch (command.getVerb()) {
49	            case "WAIT" -> Optional.empty();
50	            case "COOK", "PREPARE", "ADVANCE" -> Optional.of(cook(command));
51	            default -> throw new IllegalArgumentException(
52	                    "Unknown Chef command '" + command.getVerb() + "'. Use COOK or WAIT.");
53	        };
54	    }
55	
56	    private String seat(ManagerCommand command, BiConsumer<Customer, Table> seater, String actor) {
57	        Customer customer = command.select(restaurant.getWaitingQueue(), restaurant.getCustomers(),
58	                "nobody is in the waiting queue", "only a customer in the waiting queue can be seated");
59	        Table table = restaurant.findAvailableTable();
60	        if (table == null) {
61	            throw new IllegalStateException("Both tables are occupied; a table frees up when its customer pays or leaves");
62	        }
63	        restaurant.seatFromQueue(customer, table, seater);
64	        return actor + " seats " + customer.getDisplayName() + " at Table " + table.getTableId() + ".";
65	    }
66	
67	    private String takeOrder(ManagerCommand command, int currentTurn) {
68	        Customer customer = command.select(restaurant.getCustomersWithStatus(CustomerStatus.SEATED),
69	                restaurant.getCustomers(), "no seated customer is waiting to order", "only a seated customer can order");
70	        Order order = restaurant.getWaiter().takeOrder(customer, restaurant.getMenu(), restaurant.getHappyHour(), currentTurn);
71	        MenuItem item = order.getItem();
72	        return "Waiter takes the order of " + customer.getDisplayName() + ". They choose "
73	                + item.getName() + " (list price " + Money.format(item.getPriceCents()) + ", "
74	                + item.getPreparationUnits() + " preparation unit(s)). Bill locked at "
75	                + Money.format(order.getBillCents()) + ".";
76	    }
77	
78	    private String serve(ManagerCommand command, int currentTurn) {
79	        Customer customer = command.select(restaurant.getCustomersToServe(),
80	                restaurant.getCustomers(), "no READY order is waiting to be served",
81	                "only a customer with a READY order can be served");
82	        restaurant.getWaiter().serveOrder(customer, currentTurn);
83	        return "Waiter serves the " + customer.getOrder().getItem().getName() + " to "
84	                + customer.getDisplayName() + ".";
85	    }
86	
87	    private String cook(ManagerCommand command) {
88	        Customer customer = command.select(restaurant.getCustomersToCook(),
89	                restaurant.getCustomers(), "no unfinished order needs preparation",
90	                "only a customer with an unfinished order has food to prepare");
91	        Order order = customer.getOrder();
92	        restaurant.getChef().prepare(order);
93	        return "Chef works on the " + order.getItem().getName() + " for " + customer.getDisplayName()
94	                + ": " + order.getCompletedUnits() + "/" + order.getItem().getPreparationUnits()
95	                + " units, order is " + order.getStatus() + ".";
96	    }
97	}

1	package restaurantrush.control;
2	
3	import java.util.List;
4	import java.util.Locale;
5	
6	import restaurantrush.entity.Customer;
7	
8	/**
9	 * One typed manager choice: a verb and an optional customer number, for example "SERVE #3".
10	 * A blank line means WAIT. It reads the text and works out which customer it refers to; whether
11	 * the task is allowed is decided by the staff and the domain objects.
12	 */
13	final class ManagerCommand {
14	    private final String verb;
15	    private final Integer customerId;
16	
17	    private ManagerCommand(String verb, Integer customerId) {
18	        this.verb = verb;
19	        this.customerId = customerId;
20	    }
21	
22	    static ManagerCommand parse(String raw) {
23	        String text = raw == null ? "" : raw.trim().toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT);
24	        if (text.isEmpty()) {
25	            return new ManagerCommand("WAIT", null);
26	        }
27	        String[] words = text.split("\\s+");
28	        if (words.length > 2) {
29	            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Expected 'COMMAND [customer number]' but got '" + raw.trim() + "'");
30	        }
31	        Integer id = null;
32	        if (words.length == 2) {
33	            String number = words[1].startsWith("#") ? words[1].substring(1) : words[1];
34	            try {
35	                id = Integer.valueOf(number);
36	            } catch (NumberFormatException notANumber) {
37	                throw new IllegalArgumentException("'" + words[1] + "' is not a customer number");
38	            }
39	        }
40	        return new ManagerCommand(words[0], id);
41	    }
42	
43	    String getVerb() {
44	        return verb;
45	    }
46	
47	    /**
48	     * Resolves the customer this command refers to among those who could take part in the task, or
49	     * explains why it cannot. With no number given, a single candidate is chosen automatically.
50	     */
51	    Customer select(List<Customer> candidates, List<Customer> everyone, String noCandidates, String ineligible) {
52	        if (customerId == null) {
53	            if (candidates.isEmpty()) {
54	                throw new IllegalStateException("Nothing to do: " + noCandidates);
55	            }
56	            if (candidates.size() > 1) {
57	                throw new IllegalArgumentException("Several customers qualify (" + idList(candidates)
58	                        + "); add a customer number, for example " + verb + " " + candidates.get(0).getId());
59	            }
60	            return candidates.get(0);
61	        }
62	        for (Customer candidate : candidates) {
63	            if (candidate.getId() == customerId) {
64	                return candidate;
65	            }
66	        }
67	        for (Customer customer : everyone) {
68	            if (customer.getId() == customerId) {
69	                throw new IllegalStateException(customer.getDisplayName() + " is " + customer.getStatus()
70	                        + ": " + ineligible);
71	            }
72	        }
73	        throw new IllegalArgumentException("There is no customer #" + customerId);
74	    }
75	
76	    private static String idList(List<Customer> customers) {
77	        StringBuilder ids = new StringBuilder();
78	        for (Customer customer : customers) {
79	            ids.append(ids.length() == 0 ? "#" : ", #").append(customer.getId());
80	        }
81	        return ids.toString();
82	    }
83	}
84

1	package restaurantrush.control;
2	
3	import java.util.List;
4	import java.util.function.IntFunction;
5	
6	import restaurantrush.entity.CriticCustomer;
7	import restaurantrush.entity.Customer;
8	import restaurantrush.entity.RegularCustomer;
9	import restaurantrush.entity.VIPCustomer;
10	
11	/**
12	 * The base-setting arrivals: one customer at the start of turns 1, 4, 7, 10, 13 and 16. Customers
13	 * are numbered in arrival order. Two of the six are special: the turn-1 arrival is a VIP and the
14	 * turn-10 arrival is a Critic; the other four are Regulars.
15	 */
16	public class ArrivalSchedule {
17	    private final int[] arrivalTurns = {1, 4, 7, 10, 13, 16};
18	    private final List<IntFunction<Customer>> customerTypes = List.of(
19	            VIPCustomer::new, RegularCustomer::new, RegularCustomer::new,
20	            CriticCustomer::new, RegularCustomer::new, RegularCustomer::new);
21	    private int nextCustomerId = 1;
22	
23	    public boolean isArrivalTurn(int turn) {
24	        return indexOf(turn) >= 0;
25	    }
26	
27	    /** Creates the customer who arrives at the start of this turn. */
28	    public Customer createArrival(int turn) {
29	        int index = indexOf(turn);
30	        if (index < 0) {
31	            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Nobody arrives at the start of turn " + turn);
32	        }
33	        return customerTypes.get(index).apply(nextCustomerId++);
34	    }
35	
36	    private int indexOf(int turn) {
37	        for (int i = 0; i < arrivalTurns.length; i++) {
38	            if (arrivalTurns[i] == turn) {
39	                return i;
40	            }
41	        }
42	        return -1;
43	    }
44	}

1	package restaurantrush.entity;
2	
3	/**
4	 * The Waiter seats customers, takes their orders and serves finished dishes. The controller lets
5	 * the Waiter do at most one of these per turn; each method rejects an impossible request by
6	 * throwing, before anything is changed.
7	 */
8	public class Waiter {
9	
10	    public void seatCustomer(Customer customer, Table table) {
11	        if (customer.getStatus() != CustomerStatus.WAITING) {
12	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customer.getId() + " is " + customer.getStatus()
13	                    + "; only a customer in the waiting queue can be seated");
14	        }
15	        if (!table.isAvailable()) {
16	            throw new IllegalStateException("Table " + table.getTableId() + " is already occupied");
17	        }
18	        table.seat(customer);
19	        customer.markSeated();
20	    }
21	
22	    /**
23	     * The customer chooses the dish and works out their own bill (a VIP gets a discount); then the
24	     * Happy Hour discount, if active, is applied on top. The bill is locked in the new Order.
25	     */
26	    public Order takeOrder(Customer customer, Menu menu, HappyHour happyHour, int currentTurn) {
27	        if (customer.getStatus() != CustomerStatus.SEATED) {
28	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customer.getId() + " is " + customer.getStatus()
29	                    + "; only a seated customer can order");
30	        }
31	        MenuItem item = customer.chooseMenuItem(menu);
32	        int billCents = happyHour.applyDiscount(customer.calculateBillCents(item.getPriceCents()));
33	        Order order = new Order(item, billCents, currentTurn);
34	        customer.assignOrder(order);
35	        return order;
36	    }
37	
38	    public void serveOrder(Customer customer, int currentTurn) {
39	        Order order = customer.getOrder();
40	        if (customer.getStatus() != CustomerStatus.ORDERED || order == null) {
41	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customer.getId() + " is " + customer.getStatus()
42	                    + "; there is no order waiting to be served");
43	        }
44	        if (!order.isReady()) {
45	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customer.getId() + "'s " + order.getItem().getName()
46	                    + " is not READY yet (" + order.getStatus() + ", " + order.getCompletedUnits() + "/"
47	                    + order.getItem().getPreparationUnits() + " units)");
48	        }
49	        order.markServed();
50	        customer.markServed(currentTurn);
51	        customer.onServed(currentTurn - order.getTurnTaken());
52	    }
53	}
54

1	package restaurantrush.entity;
2	
3	/**
4	 * One customer's order. The status is private and moves only through the methods below, so an
5	 * illegal jump (for example serving a dish that is not READY) is rejected here, not in the UI.
6	 * Legal: PLACED -> PREPARING -> READY -> SERVED -> PAID, with PLACED -> READY for a 1-unit dish,
7	 * and PLACED / PREPARING / READY -> CANCELLED when the customer leaves.
8	 */
9	public class Order {
10	    private final MenuItem item;
11	    private int completedUnits;
12	    private OrderStatus status;
13	    private final int billCents;
14	    private final int turnTaken;
15	
16	    /** The bill is fixed here and has no setter: the price is locked when the order is taken. */
17	    public Order(MenuItem item, int billCents, int turnTaken) {
18	        this.item = item;
19	        this.billCents = billCents;
20	        this.turnTaken = turnTaken;
21	        this.status = OrderStatus.PLACED;
22	    }
23	
24	    /** The Chef spends one preparation unit; the last unit makes the order READY. */
25	    public void advancePreparation() {
26	        if (status != OrderStatus.PLACED && status != OrderStatus.PREPARING) {
27	            throw new IllegalStateException("The " + item.getName() + " order is " + status
28	                    + " and cannot be prepared any further");
29	        }
30	        completedUnits++;
31	        status = completedUnits >= item.getPreparationUnits() ? OrderStatus.READY : OrderStatus.PREPARING;
32	    }
33	
34	    public boolean isReady() {
35	        return status == OrderStatus.READY;
36	    }
37	
38	    public void markServed() {
39	        require(OrderStatus.READY, "served");
40	        status = OrderStatus.SERVED;
41	    }
42	
43	    public void markPaid() {
44	        require(OrderStatus.SERVED, "paid");
45	        status = OrderStatus.PAID;
46	    }
47	
48	    public void cancel() {
49	        if (status != OrderStatus.PLACED && status != OrderStatus.PREPARING && status != OrderStatus.READY) {
50	            throw new IllegalStateException("A " + status + " order cannot be cancelled");
51	        }
52	        status = OrderStatus.CANCELLED;
53	    }
54	
55	    public OrderStatus getStatus() {
56	        return status;
57	    }
58	
59	    public MenuItem getItem() {
60	        return item;
61	    }
62	
63	    public int getCompletedUnits() {
64	        return completedUnits;
65	    }
66	
67	    public int getBillCents() {
68	        return billCents;
69	    }
70	
71	    public int getTurnTaken() {
72	        return turnTaken;
73	    }
74	
75	    private void require(OrderStatus expected, String action) {
76	        if (status != expected) {
77	            throw new IllegalStateException("The " + item.getName() + " order is " + status
78	                    + "; only a " + expected + " order can be " + action);
79	        }
80	    }
81	}
82

1	package restaurantrush.entity;
2	
3	/**
4	 * A pricing policy the manager may switch on once per game, at the start of a turn. It lasts for
5	 * that turn and the next. While active, orders taken get 20% off and customers still waiting for
6	 * a table or food recover 15 satisfaction at the start of the turn. It never changes an order
7	 * that is already placed, because the bill is locked in the Order.
8	 */
9	public class HappyHour {
10	    private static final int DISCOUNT_PERCENT = 20;
11	    private static final int RECOVERY = 15;
12	    private static final int DURATION = 2;
13	
14	    private boolean used;
15	    private int remainingTurns;
16	
17	    /** Switches Happy Hour on. Returns false (and changes nothing) if it was already used this game. */
18	    public boolean activate() {
19	        if (used) {
20	            return false;
21	        }
22	        used = true;
23	        remainingTurns = DURATION;
24	        return true;
25	    }
26	
27	    public boolean isActive() {
28	        return remainingTurns > 0;
29	    }
30	
31	    /** True until the manager switches Happy Hour on; it cannot be used a second time. */
32	    public boolean isAvailable() {
33	        return !used;
34	    }
35	
36	    public int getRemainingTurns() {
37	        return remainingTurns;
38	    }
39	
40	    /** The bill after the Happy Hour discount (rounded to the nearest cent), or unchanged when it is not active. */
41	    public int applyDiscount(int cents) {
42	        if (!isActive()) {
43	            return cents;
44	        }
45	        return (cents * (100 - DISCOUNT_PERCENT) + 50) / 100;
46	    }
47	
48	    /** Satisfaction a waiting customer recovers at the start of an active turn; 0 when not active. */
49	    public int getRecoveryAmount() {
50	        return isActive() ? RECOVERY : 0;
51	    }
52	
53	    /** Called once at the end of every turn. */
54	    public void endTurn() {
55	        if (remainingTurns > 0) {
56	            remainingTurns--;
57	        }
58	    }
59	}
60

package restaurantrush.entity;

/**
 * The Host seats at most one queued customer per turn, in addition to whatever the Waiter does
 * (the Waiter can still seat customers too). The controller allows one Host task per turn.
 */
public class Host {

    /** Rejects an occupied table or a customer who is not in the waiting queue, before changing anything. */
    public void seatCustomer(Customer customer, Table table) {
        if (!table.isAvailable()) {
            throw new IllegalStateException("Table " + table.getTableId() + " is already occupied");
        }
        customer.markSeated();
        table.seat(customer);
    }
}
=============== entity/ComboMeal.java
package restaurantrush.entity;

import java.util.List;

/**
 * A combo is a menu item made of two existing menu items. It costs 90% of their combined price
 * (rounded to the nearest cent) and takes the sum of their preparation units, so the Waiter, Chef
 * and Cashier treat it like any other dish.
 */
public class ComboMeal extends MenuItem {
    private static final int PRICE_PERCENT = 90;

    private final MenuItem first;
    private final MenuItem second;

    public ComboMeal(MenuItem first, MenuItem second) {
        super(first.getName() + " + " + second.getName(),
                ((first.getPriceCents() + second.getPriceCents()) * PRICE_PERCENT + 50) / 100,
                first.getPreparationUnits() + second.getPreparationUnits());
        this.first = first;
        this.second = second;
    }

    public List<MenuItem> getComponents() {
        return List.of(first, second);
    }
}
=============== entity/MenuItem.java
package restaurantrush.entity;

/** A dish on the menu. Immutable: name, price in cents and the preparation units the Chef must spend. */
public class MenuItem {
    private final String name;
    private final int priceCents;
    private final int preparationUnits;

    public MenuItem(String name, int priceCents, int preparationUnits) {
        if (name == null || name.isBlank()) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("A menu item needs a name");
        }
        if (priceCents <= 0 || preparationUnits <= 0) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Price and preparation units must be positive");
        }
        this.name = name;
        this.priceCents = priceCents;
        this.preparationUnits = preparationUnits;
    }

    public String getName() {
        return name;
    }

    public int getPriceCents() {
        return priceCents;
    }

    public int getPreparationUnits() {
        return preparationUnits;
    }
}
=============== entity/Menu.java
package restaurantrush.entity;

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.Collections;
import java.util.List;

/** The dishes customers can order. Customers choose from it; the manager never does. */
public class Menu {
    private final List<MenuItem> items;

    public Menu(List<MenuItem> items) {
        if (items == null || items.isEmpty()) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("A menu needs at least one item");
        }
        this.items = new ArrayList<>(items);
    }

    public List<MenuItem> getItems() {
        return Collections.unmodifiableList(items);
    }

    public MenuItem getItem(int index) {
        return items.get(index);
    }

    public int size() {
        return items.size();
    }

    /** The item with the highest price (the first one listed if prices tie). */
    public MenuItem getMostExpensive() {
        MenuItem best = items.get(0);
        for (MenuItem item : items) {
            if (item.getPriceCents() > best.getPriceCents()) {
                best = item;
            }
        }
        return best;
    }
}
=============== entity/Table.java
package restaurantrush.entity;

/** A table that holds at most one customer. It stays occupied until that customer pays or leaves. */
public class Table {
    private final int tableId;
    private Customer occupant;

    public Table(int tableId) {
        this.tableId = tableId;
    }

    public int getTableId() {
        return tableId;
    }

    public boolean isAvailable() {
        return occupant == null;
    }

    public void seat(Customer customer) {
        if (!isAvailable()) {
            throw new IllegalStateException("Table " + tableId + " is already occupied");
        }
        occupant = customer;
    }

    public void release() {
        occupant = null;
    }

    public Customer getOccupied() {
        return occupant;
    }
}
=============== entity/Cashier.java
package restaurantrush.entity;

/** The Cashier collects the bill of one READY_TO_PAY customer, records the revenue and frees the table. */
public class Cashier {

    public void processPayment(Customer customer, Restaurant restaurant) {
        Order order = customer.getOrder();
        if (customer.getStatus() != CustomerStatus.READY_TO_PAY || order == null) {
            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customer.getId() + " is " + customer.getStatus()
                    + "; the Cashier can only charge a READY_TO_PAY customer");
        }
        restaurant.recordPayment(order.getBillCents());
        customer.markPaid();
        order.markPaid();
        restaurant.releaseTableOf(customer);
    }
}
=============== entity/Chef.java
package restaurantrush.entity;

/** The Chef spends one preparation unit on an order per turn (the controller allows one choice per turn). */
public class Chef {

    /** Advances the order by one unit; an order that is READY or cancelled is rejected by the Order. */
    public void prepare(Order order) {
        order.advancePreparation();
    }
}
=============== entity/VIPCustomer.java
package restaurantrush.entity;

/** A VIP is more patient (loses 5 per waiting turn) and pays 90% of the list price. */
public class VIPCustomer extends Customer {
    private static final int SATISFACTION_LOSS = 5;
    private static final int PRICE_PERCENT = 90;

    public VIPCustomer(int customerId) {
        super(customerId);
    }

    @Override
    public String getTypeLabel() {
        return "VIP";
    }

    @Override
    protected int getSatisfactionLoss() {
        return SATISFACTION_LOSS;
    }

    /** 10% off, rounded to the nearest cent. */
    @Override
    public int calculateBillCents(int basePriceCents) {
        return (basePriceCents * PRICE_PERCENT + 50) / 100;
    }
}
=============== entity/CriticCustomer.java
package restaurantrush.entity;

/**
 * A demanding customer: loses 12 per waiting turn, always orders the most expensive dish, and is
 * further upset (-10) when served more than 2 turns after the order was taken.
 */
public class CriticCustomer extends Customer {
    private static final int SATISFACTION_LOSS = 12;
    private static final int LATE_SERVICE_TURNS = 2;
    private static final int LATE_PENALTY = 10;

    public CriticCustomer(int customerId) {
        super(customerId);
    }

    @Override
    public String getTypeLabel() {
        return "Critic";
    }

    @Override
    protected int getSatisfactionLoss() {
        return SATISFACTION_LOSS;
    }

    @Override
    public MenuItem chooseMenuItem(Menu menu) {
        return menu.getMostExpensive();
    }

    @Override
    public void onServed(int turnsWaited) {
        if (turnsWaited > LATE_SERVICE_TURNS) {
            adjustSatisfaction(-LATE_PENALTY);
        }
    }
}
=============== entity/RegularCustomer.java
package restaurantrush.entity;

/** The baseline customer: full price, loses 8 satisfaction per waiting turn. */
public class RegularCustomer extends Customer {
    private static final int SATISFACTION_LOSS = 8;

    public RegularCustomer(int customerId) {
        super(customerId);
    }

    @Override
    public String getTypeLabel() {
        return "Regular";
    }

    @Override
    protected int getSatisfactionLoss() {
        return SATISFACTION_LOSS;
    }
}
=============== entity/Money.java
package restaurantrush.entity;

import java.util.Locale;

/** Money is kept as integer cents everywhere; this only formats it as dollars with two decimals. */
public final class Money {
    private Money() {
    }

    public static String format(int cents) {
        return String.format(Locale.ROOT, "$%d.%02d", cents / 100, cents % 100);
    }
}

package restaurantrush.entity;

/** Where a customer is in the visit: queue, table, order, meal, payment, or gone. */
public enum CustomerStatus {
    WAITING,
    SEATED,
    ORDERED,
    SERVED,
    READY_TO_PAY,
    PAID,
    ABANDONED
}

/** Where an order is between the Waiter taking it and the Cashier collecting payment. */
public enum OrderStatus {
    PLACED,
    PREPARING,
    READY,
    SERVED,
    PAID,
